) are satisfied, and the images of the maps
\varepsilon_{1}
and
{\omega}^{\mathrm{sym}}
lie in the kernel of the VB morphism
\sharp^{C}
.

Proof.

This corollary follows directly from Theorem
3.16
and Remark
3.14
:

Assume
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
is an AL HA. Then
{\psi}^{\mathrm{alt}}=0
follows from (

AL
ψ

), and the identity

\sharp^{C}(\beta(s_{1},s_{2}))=\sharp[s_{1},s_{2}]

follows from (
3.37
) and (

AL
A

). Therefore,

\frac{1}{2}\sharp^{C}\circ\varepsilon_{1}=\sharp^{C}\circ\beta-\sharp^{C}\circ\partial\circ[\cdot,\cdot]=(\sharp-\sharp^{C}\circ\partial)\circ[\cdot,\cdot]=0
, due to (

AL
∂

).
Clearly,

\sharp^{C}\circ{\omega}^{\mathrm{sym}}=0.

follows from (

AL
ω

).

Conversely, from Theorem
3.16
and Remark
3.14
, it follows that the maps listed in Corollary
3.17
define a skew HA (with
{\psi}^{\mathrm{sym}}=0
).

The identity (
3.2.4
) holds by the assumption
\sharp^{C}\circ\varepsilon_{1}=0
, (

AL
A

), and (

AL
∂

). Hence
{\psi}^{\mathrm{alt}}=0
, and (

AL
ψ

) follows from the assumption
{\omega}^{\mathrm{sym}}=0
.

We have,
\sharp^{C}\circ{\omega}^{\mathrm{alt}}(s_{1},s_{2},s)=\frac{1}{2}\sharp\circ\operatorname{Jac}(s_{1},s_{2},s)
, from
(
3.28
), (
3.2.4
), and (

AL
□

), where

\operatorname{Jac}(X_{1},X_{2},X)=[X_{1},[X_{2},X]]-[X_{2},[X_{1},X]]-[[X_{1},X_{2}],X].

(3.46)

Moreover,
\sharp\circ\operatorname{Jac}(s_{1},s_{2},s)=\operatorname{Jac}(\sharp s_{1},\sharp s_{2},\sharp s)=0
since
A
is an AL algebroid. Therefore,
\sharp^{C}\circ{\omega}^{\mathrm{alt}}=0
and (

AL
ω

) follows from the assumption

.

It is well known that if

3.2.5
Lie HAs of order two

In the following result, we provide conditions (referred to as the axioms of Lie HAs) on the structure maps introduced earlier, ensuring that a given AL HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
is a Lie HA.

Theorem 3.20

(Lie HAs of order two)
.

Let
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
be an AL HA. Then
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
is Lie if and only if

A
is a Lie algebroid,

(
\mathrm{Lie_{A}}
)

\displaystyle\Box_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v=\Box_{s_{1}}\Box_{s_{2}}v-\Box_{s_{2}}\Box_{s_{1}}v,

(
\mathrm{Lie_{\Box}}
)

\displaystyle\partial([s_{1},s_{2}])=\Box_{s_{1}}\partial(s_{2}),\text{i.e., $\varepsilon_{0}=0$,}

(
\mathrm{Lie_{\partial}}
)

\displaystyle\beta(s_{1},s_{2})=\partial([s_{1},s_{2}]),\text{i.e., $\varepsilon_{1}=0$,}

(
\mathrm{Lie_{\beta}}
)

\displaystyle\omega=0.

(
\mathrm{Lie_{\omega}}
)

Remark 3.21
.

The condition (

Lie
ω

) can be replaced with

{\omega}^{\mathrm{sym}}=0.

(
\mathrm{Lie_{\bar{\omega}}}
)

¯

).

Remark 3.22
.

It is a straightforward calculation to show that, in an AL HA, the mapping
\mathrm{curv}_{\Box}(s_{1},s_{2},v):=\Box_{s_{1}}\Box_{s_{2}}v-\Box_{s_{2}}\Box_{s_{1}}v-\Box_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v

on sections from local frames of the VBs

.

Proof.

The case

(We have used the compatibility of algebroid lifts with respect to

The latter is

(

We know that
\omega=0
, hence

.
For

We take

. Vanishing of

.

We follow a similar idea in the case
(i,j)=(0,0)
. Consider
\heartsuit={[s_{1},s_{2}]}^{\langle{0}\rangle}-{[s_{1},s_{2}]}^{\langle{0}\rangle}
, so

, and refer to the exact sequence (
4.1
) in Lemma

. We aim to show that

is in the kernel of the projection

. Indeed, the vector fields

(as they have weight

and

), so

. Thus,

. Analogously to the case
(i,j)=(-1,0)
, we have

, as

. Hence we know, that

, i.e., it has a form

for some functions

on

. Next, we notice that for any section

Indeed,
[{[s_{1},s_{2}]}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i,j)=(0,-1)}}{{=}}{[[s_{1},s_{2}],s]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}
and

\begin{split}[[{s_{1}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}],{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]=[{s_{1}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},[{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]]-[{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},[{s_{1}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i,j)=(0,-1)}}{{=}}\\
[{s_{1}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{[s_{2},s]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]-[{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle},{[s_{1},s]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]={[s_{1},[s_{2},s]]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}-{[s_{2},[s_{1},s]]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}={[[s_{1},s_{2}],s]}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}.\end{split}

Therefore,

for any

. On the other hand, for any

, we have

up to isomorphisms given in Lemma

. Therefore,

3.2.6
HAs of order two and representations up to homotopy of Lie algebroids

The notion of the representation up to homotopy of Lie algebroids was introduced in
[
AC12
]
. Some recollection on this subject is given in Appendix, Subsection
4.4
. In our case of interest (2-term representations), the definition given in
[
AC12
]
boils down to the following data:
a Lie algebroid
(A\rightarrow M,[\cdot,\cdot],\sharp)
,
a 2-term complex
F_{0}\xrightarrow{\partial}F_{1}
of vector bundles over
M
concentrated in degrees
0
and
1
,

A
-connections
\nabla^{i}
on
F_{i}
, for
i=0,1
, and

A
-form
K\in\Omega^{2}(A;\operatorname{Hom}(F_{1},F_{0}))
such that

(i)

\nabla_{s}^{1}\circ\partial={\partial}\circ\nabla_{s}^{0}:\operatorname{\Gamma}(F_{0})\rightarrow\operatorname{\Gamma}(F_{1})

for any
s\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
;

(ii)

\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{0}}=-K\circ\partial
, and
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{1}}=-\partial\circ K
where
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla}
denotes the curvature of an
A
-connection
\nabla
, see (
4.22
);

(iii)

the covariant derivative of
K
vanishes, i.e.,
\mathrm{d}_{\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}}K=0
where
\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}
is the
A
-connection on
\operatorname{Hom}(F_{1},F_{0})
induced by
\nabla^{0}
and
\nabla^{1}
, see (
4.25
).

(Note that
K\circ\partial=K\bm{\wedge}\partial
and
\partial\circ K=\partial\bm{\wedge}K
, where
\bm{\wedge}
denotes an operation on
A
-forms induced by the composition of maps, see (
4.23
).) All this data can be gathered together to a so called
the structure operator

D:\Omega(A;F)\to\Omega(A;F)
,
determined by the triple
(\partial,\nabla=(\nabla^{0},\nabla^{1}),K)
(also denoted by
D
) defined by means of the wedge product, as
D:=\widehat{\partial}+d_{\nabla}+\widehat{K}
, see (
4.28
).
The compatibility conditions (
i
) - (
iii
) can be shortly written as
D\circ D=0
, see Appendix.
A morphism
(E_{0}\xrightarrow{\partial^{E}}E_{1};\nabla^{E},K^{E})
to
(F_{0}\xrightarrow{\partial^{F}}F_{1};\nabla^{F},K^{F})
consists of a morphism of complexes
\Phi_{0}:(E,\partial^{E})\to(F,\partial^{F})
(i.e.,
\Phi_{0}\circ\partial^{E}=\partial^{F}\circ\Phi_{0}
) and a 1-form
\Phi_{1}\in\Omega^{1}(A;\operatorname{Hom}(E_{1},F_{0}))
such that

(i)

\partial^{\operatorname{Hom}}\Phi_{1}+\mathrm{d}_{\nabla}\Phi_{0}=0
,

(ii)

\mathrm{d}_{\nabla}\Phi_{1}+K^{F}\bm{\wedge}\Phi_{0}-\Phi_{0}\bm{\wedge}K^{E}=0
.

These conditions can be shortened to
[\widehat{\Phi},D]=0

and can be rewritten in a more explicit form as

{-}\Phi_{1}(s;\partial e)-\Phi_{0}(\nabla_{s}^{E_{0}}e)+\nabla_{s}^{F_{0}}\Phi_{0}(e)=0\text{ for $e\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E_{0})$;}

(3.48)

{-}\partial(\Phi_{1}(s;v))-\Phi_{0}(\nabla_{s}^{E_{1}}v)+\nabla_{s}^{F_{1}}\Phi_{0}(v)=0\text{ for $v\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E_{1})$};

(3.49)

\begin{split}K^{F}(s_{1},s_{2};\Phi_{0}(v))-\Phi_{0}(K^{E}(s_{1},s_{2};v))-\Phi_{1}([s_{1},s_{2}];v){-}\Phi_{1}(s_{2};\nabla_{s_{1}}^{E_{1}}v)+\nabla_{s_{1}}^{F_{0}}\Phi_{1}(s_{2};v)\\
{+}\Phi_{1}(s_{1};\nabla_{s_{2}}^{E_{1}}v)-\nabla^{F_{0}}_{s_{2}}\Phi_{1}(s_{1};v)=0,\text{ for }s_{1},s_{2}\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A),v\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E_{1}).\end{split}

(3.50)

The advantage of the framework of representations u.t.h. of Lie algebroids is that it is more flexible and contains generalizations of some important concepts from the theory of Lie algebras. The example is the adjoint representation.
It is modelled on the complex

A\xrightarrow{\sharp}\mathrm{T}M,

and the
A
-connections on this complex is induced by a linear connection
\nabla:(X,s)\mapsto\nabla_{X}s
on the vector bundle
A\to M
in the following way
14
14

14

Two choices of connections on
A
leads to isomorphic representations.

\displaystyle\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\sharp s_{2}}s_{1}+[s_{1},s_{2}],

(3.51)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}_{s}X

\displaystyle=\sharp(\nabla_{X}s)+[\sharp s,X]_{\tau_{M}}.

(3.52)

The curvatures of the
A
-connections
\nabla^{A}
and
\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}
are expressed in the terms of the following
2
-form, called
the basic curvature

R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}\in\Omega^{2}(A;\operatorname{Hom}(\mathrm{T}M,A))
, as
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{A}}=-R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}\circ\sharp
,
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}}=-\sharp\circ R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}
, where

R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}(s_{1},s_{2};X)=\nabla_{X}[s_{1},s_{2}]-[\nabla_{X}s_{1},s_{2}]-[s_{1},\nabla_{X}s_{2}]-\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}M}X}s_{1}+\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}M}X}s_{2},

(3.53)

see
[
AC12
]
.
The structure operator for the adjoint representation of a Lie algebroid is denoted by
\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla}=(\sharp,(\nabla^{A},\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}),R^{\mathrm{bas}})
.

From order-two Lie HA to 2-term representations.

Let
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
be a Lie HA of order two. Recall that it is determined by the Lie algebroid structure on the vector bundle
A\rightarrow M
(being the order-one reduction of
E^{2}
), and the structure maps
\partial
,
\Box
,
\sharp^{C}
, see Remark
3.23
. We shall define a Lie algebroid representation u.t.h. on the complex

A\xrightarrow{\partial}C
,

A
in degree 0,
C
– degree 1.
Our construction mimics the adjoint representation of a Lie algebroid.

Definition 3.24
.

Let us choose a linear connection
\nabla
on the vector bundle
\sigma:A\rightarrow M
and define:

•

an
A
-connection
\nabla^{C}
on
C
:

\nabla^{C}_{s}v:=\Box_{s}v+\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(v)}s,

where
s\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
,
v\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
;

•

an
A
-connection
\nabla^{A}
on
A
:

\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}{s_{2}}:=\nabla_{\sharp s_{2}}s_{1}+[s_{1},s_{2}];

•

an two-form
K\in\Omega^{2}(A,\operatorname{Hom}(C,A))
:

K(s_{1},s_{2};v):=\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}[s_{1},s_{2}]-[\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{1},s_{2}]-[s_{1},\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}]-\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(\nabla_{s_{2}}^{C}v)}s_{1}+\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}v)}s_{2}.

We assume that in both constructions, the adjoint representation and the representation on the complex
\partial:A\rightarrow C
, we have chosen the same linear connection on
A
. Then, the
A
-connections
\nabla^{A}
defined above and in the adjoint representation, also coincide. Moreover,

K=R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}\circ\sharp^{C},

(3.54)

where
R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}
is given in (
3.53
).
Indeed, by comparing the formulas for
K
and
R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}
, for (
3.54
) we need to show that

\sharp^{C}\circ\nabla^{C}_{s}v=\nabla_{s}^{\mathrm{T}M}(\sharp^{C}v)

(3.55)

This can be rewritten as

\sharp^{C}(\Box_{s}v)+\sharp^{C}\circ\partial\,\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s=\sharp\left(\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s\right)+[\sharp s,\sharp^{C}v]

and it is true due to the AL assumption (see (

AL
□

), (

AL
∂

) in Theorem
3.16
).

Lemma 3.25
.

An order-two Lie higher algebroid
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})

gives rise, as explained in Definition
3.24
, to a representation u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
(the order-one reduction of
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
) on the complex

A\xrightarrow{\partial}C

(3.56)

with the structure operator given by
D=(\partial,(\nabla^{A},\nabla^{C}),K)
. Two choices of the connection on the vector bundle
\sigma:A\rightarrow M
result in isomorphic representations. Moreover,
\operatorname{id}_{A}\oplus\sharp^{C}
gives rise to a morphism from the constructed representation
(A_{[0]}\oplus C_{[1]},D)
to the adjoint representation
(A_{[0]}\oplus(\mathrm{T}M)_{[1]},\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla})
of
A
.

Proof.

It is straightforward to check that
\nabla^{C}
is an
A
-connection. Indeed, using tensor-like properties of
\Box
described in Theorem
3.13
, we get

\nabla^{C}_{fs}v={f(\Box_{s}v)}-(\sharp^{C}v)(f)\,\partial s+f\,\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s+\partial\left((\sharp^{C}v)(f)\,s\right)=f\nabla_{s}^{C}v.

Similarly, we check that
\nabla^{C}_{s}:\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)\rightarrow\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
is a derivative endomorphism,

\nabla^{C}_{s}(fv)-f\nabla^{C}_{s}v=\Box_{fs}v-f\Box_{s}v+\partial\circ\left(\nabla_{f\sharp^{c}v}s-f\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s\right)=(\sharp s)(f)v.

The
A
-connections
\nabla^{A}
and
\nabla^{C}
are compatible with
\partial:A\to C
. Indeed,

\partial\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}=\partial\left([s_{1},s_{2}]+\nabla_{\sharp s_{2}}s_{1}\right)=\Box_{s_{1}}(\partial s_{2})+\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(\partial s_{2})}s_{1}=\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}{\partial s_{2}}.

Here, we used (

AL
∂

) and (

Lie
∂

) which are true in any Lie HA. In analogy to
[
AC12
, Proposition 2.11]
we shall prove that

(i)

\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{A}}=-K\circ\partial
and
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{C}}=-\partial\circ K
;

(ii)

\mathrm{d}_{\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}}K=0
, i.e.,
K
is closed with respect to the
A
-connection
\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}
on
\operatorname{Hom}(C,A)
induced by
\nabla^{A}
and
\nabla^{C}
.

Recall that the curvature of
\nabla^{A}
is
-R^{\mathrm{bas}}\circ\sharp
, hence

\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{A}}(s_{1},s_{2};s)=-R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp s)=-R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}(\partial s)),

i.e.,
\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{A}}=-(R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}\circ\sharp^{C})\circ\partial=-K\circ\partial
due to (
3.54
).
For the curvature of
\nabla^{C}
we apply
\partial
to (
3.53
) and get

\displaystyle\partial\circ R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}v)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}[s_{1},s_{2}]}_{\mathrm{I}}-\underbrace{\partial[\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{1},s_{2}]}_{\mathrm{II}}-\underbrace{\partial[s_{1},\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}]}_{\mathrm{II}^{\prime}}-\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}M}\sharp^{C}v}s_{1}}_{\mathrm{III}}+\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}M}\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}}_{\mathrm{III}^{\prime}}

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\nabla^{C}_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Box_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v}_{\mathrm{J}}+\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}[s_{1},s_{2}]}_{\mathrm{I}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}\nabla_{s_{2}}^{C}v

\displaystyle=\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}\left(\Box_{s_{2}}v+\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Box_{s_{1}}\Box_{s_{2}}v}_{\mathrm{J}^{\prime}}+\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(\Box_{s_{2}}v)}s_{1}}_{\mathrm{III}_{1}}+\underbrace{\Box_{s_{1}}\left(\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}\right)}_{\mathrm{II}}+\underbrace{\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(\partial\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2})}\,s_{1}}_{\mathrm{III}_{2}}

We have analogous expressions
(\mathrm{J}^{\prime\prime})
,
(\mathrm{III}_{1}^{\prime})
,
(\mathrm{II}^{\prime})
and
(\mathrm{III}_{2}^{\prime})
for
\nabla_{s_{2}}^{C}\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}v
. We should show that

\partial\circ R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}v)+\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}\nabla_{s_{2}}^{C}v-\nabla_{s_{2}}^{C}\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}v-\nabla^{C}_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v=0.

We see that the expressions
(\mathrm{J})
,
(\mathrm{J}^{\prime})
and
(\mathrm{J}^{\prime\prime})

cancel, due to (

Lie
□

). Similarly for the two expressions denoted by
(\mathrm{I})
. Next, the expressions
(\mathrm{II})
cancel due to (

Lie
∂

), and similarly for
(\mathrm{II}^{\prime})
. Finally, for
(\mathrm{III})
we have

\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}M}\sharp^{C}v}\,s_{1}=\underbrace{\nabla_{\sharp\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}}\,s_{1}}_{\mathrm{III}_{2}}+\underbrace{\nabla_{[\sharp s_{2},\sharp^{C}v]}s_{1}}_{\mathrm{III}_{1}},

hence
(\mathrm{III})
equals
(\mathrm{III}_{1})+(\mathrm{III}_{2})
. Similarly,
(\mathrm{III}^{\prime})
cancels with the sum of
(\mathrm{III}_{1}^{\prime})
and
(\mathrm{III}_{2}^{\prime})
. Here we used Theorem
3.16
: (

AL
□

) and (

AL
∂

).

We shall prove the second claim that the 2-form
K
is closed. We shall use (
3.54
) and the equality
\mathrm{d}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{bas}}}R^{\mathrm{bas}}=0
which is proved in
[
AC12
]
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}}K(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3};v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\text{cyclic}}\left(\nabla^{\operatorname{Hom}}_{s_{1}}K(s_{2},s_{3})\right)(v)-K([s_{1},s_{2}],s_{3};v)=

\displaystyle=\sum_{\text{cyclic}}\underbrace{\nabla_{s_{1}}^{A}\left(K(s_{2},s_{3};v)\right)}_{R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{2},s_{3};\sharp^{C}v)}-\underbrace{K(s_{2},s_{3})(\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}v)}_{R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{2},s_{3})(\sharp^{C}\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}v)}-R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}([s_{1},s_{2}],s_{3};\sharp^{C}v)=

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{bas}}}R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3};\sharp^{C}v)=0,

by (
3.55
).
The proof that
D=(\partial,\nabla,K)
is a structure operator is completed.

Let us assume that we have chosen two linear connections
\nabla
and
\widetilde{\nabla}
on the vector bundle
\sigma:A\rightarrow M
. We define
\Phi_{0}=\operatorname{id}_{A\oplus C}
and
\Phi_{1}(s)(v)=\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s-\widetilde{\nabla}_{\sharp^{C}v}s
,
\Phi_{1}\in\Omega^{1}(A,\operatorname{Hom}(C,A))
. Note that
\operatorname{Hom}(C,A)=\operatorname{End}^{-1}(A_{[0]}\oplus C_{[1]})
. Then
\Phi_{0}+\Phi_{1}
establishes an isomorphism between the representations u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
, induced from a given HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
, defined by means of the linear connections
\nabla
and
\widetilde{\nabla}
, respectively. Indeed, the equation (
3.48
) writes as

\Phi_{1}(s_{1};\partial s_{2})=\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}-\widetilde{\nabla}^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}.

The RHS is
\nabla_{\sharp s_{2}}s_{1}-\widetilde{\nabla}_{\sharp s_{2}}s_{1}
and the same is LHS as

\sharp^{C}\circ\partial=\sharp
. The second equation (
3.49
) writes as

\partial(\Phi_{1}(s;v))=\nabla^{C}_{s}v-\widetilde{\nabla}^{C}_{s}v

and both sides are equal to
\partial\circ\left(\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s-\widetilde{\nabla}^{C}_{\sharp^{C}v}s\right)
due to the definitions of
\Phi_{1}
and the
A
-connection on
C
(see Definition
3.24
). The third equation (
3.50
) is a consequence of a similar result for the adjoint representation. Namely, if
\Psi_{0}=\operatorname{id}_{A\oplus\mathrm{T}M}
,
\Psi_{1}(s;X)=\nabla_{X}s-\widetilde{\nabla}_{X}s
,
\Psi_{1}\in\Omega^{1}(A,\underline{\operatorname{End}}^{-1}(A\oplus\mathrm{T}M))
, then
\Psi_{0}+\Psi_{1}
is an isomorphism
(A\oplus\mathrm{T}M,\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla})\rightarrow(A\oplus\mathrm{T}M,\operatorname{ad}_{\widetilde{\nabla}})
between the adjoint representations of the Lie algebroid
A
associated with the linear connections
\nabla
and
\widetilde{\nabla}
, respectively. We have

\Phi_{1}(s;v)=\Psi_{1}(s;\sharp^{C}v),

hence, from (
3.54
) and (
3.55
), we find that

\Phi_{1}(s_{2};\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}v)=\Psi_{1}(s_{2};\sharp^{C}\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}v)=\Psi_{1}(s_{2};\nabla_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}M}\sharp^{C}v).

Hence, (
3.50
) can be written in our case as:

\displaystyle K_{\widetilde{\nabla}}(s_{1},s_{2};v)-K_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};v)=\underbrace{\Phi_{1}([s_{1},s_{2}];v)}_{\Psi_{1}([s_{1},s_{2}];\sharp^{C}v)}+\Phi_{1}(s_{2};\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}v){-}\Phi_{1}(s_{1};\nabla^{C}_{s_{2}}v)+

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}^{A}_{s_{2}}(\Phi_{1}(s_{1};v))-\widetilde{\nabla}^{A}_{s_{1}}(\Phi_{1}(s_{2};v)),

and it follows from the same equation (
3.50
) applied to the adjoint representation, i.e., with
K
,
v
, and
\Phi
replaced with
R^{\mathrm{bas}}
,
\sharp^{C}v
, and
\Psi
, respectively.

For the last statement, we clearly see that
\Phi_{0}=(\operatorname{id}_{A},\sharp^{C}):A\oplus C\to A\oplus\mathrm{T}M
is a morphism of complexes, due to
\sharp=\sharp^{C}\circ\partial
(see Theorem
3.16
). We set
\Phi_{1}=0
and find that equation (
3.48
) holds automatically, equation (
3.49
) is true due to (
3.55
), and (
3.50
) reduces to (
3.54
).
∎

Recovering HA.

Assume we are given a Lie algebroid
(A\to M,[\cdot,\cdot],\sharp)
, the structure operator
D=(\partial,(\nabla^{A},\nabla^{C}),K)
, which provides a representation u.t.h. of
A
on the complex
\partial:A\to C
. Let
\Phi
be a morphism to the adjoint representation
(A,\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla})
, where
\nabla
is a chosen linear connection on
A
. We assume that
\Phi_{0}|_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
and
\Phi_{1}=0
, where
\Phi_{0}
,
\Phi_{1}
are the components of
\Phi
:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sharp^{C}:=\Phi_{0}|_{C}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sharp}
\textstyle{\mathrm{T}M}

We shall show how to recover the structure of a Lie HA on the graded bundle
E^{2}
constructed in Lemma
3.6
by means of the VB morphism
\partial:A\to C
given already.
The structure map
\sharp^{C}
of the HA are taken from the diagram above, as
\sharp^{C}=\Phi_{0}|_{C}
.
The structure map
\Box:\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)\times\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)\rightarrow\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
is recovered by means of the formula given in Definition
3.24
,

\Box_{s}v=\nabla^{C}_{s}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(v)}s.

We easily check the tensor-like properties of the action
(s,v)\mapsto\Box_{s}v
:

•

\Box_{fs}v-f(\Box_{s}v)=-\partial\circ(\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}(fs)-f\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s)=-(\sharp^{C}v)(f)\,\partial(s)
,
as
\nabla_{s}^{C}v
is
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear in
s
.

•

\Box_{s}(fv)-f\,(\Box_{s}v)=\nabla^{C}_{s}(fv)-f\nabla_{s}^{C}v=(\sharp s)(f)\,v
due to the properties of
A
-connections.

We shall show that the compatibility conditions given in Theorem
3.20
, which ensure Lie HA structure are satisfied.

Obviously, (

AL
∂

) is true due to the commutativity of the diagram above.
Since
\Phi_{1}=0
the conditions (
3.48
), (
3.49
), (
3.50
) simplify to:

(i)

The
A
-connections on the vector bundle
\sigma:A\to M
, being part of the structure operators
D
and
\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla}
, coincide;

(ii)

\sharp^{C}\left(\nabla^{C}_{s}v\right)=\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}_{s}\left(\sharp^{C}v\right)
;

(iii)

R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}v)=K(s_{1},s_{2};v)
.

We have

\sharp^{C}(\Box_{s}v)=\sharp^{C}\left(\nabla^{C}_{s}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s\right)=\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}_{s}\left(\sharp^{C}v\right)-(\sharp^{C}\circ\partial)\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s=[\sharp s,\sharp^{C}v]

due to the formula (
3.52
) for
\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}
. It proves (

AL
□

).
Next,

\begin{split}\Box_{s_{1}}\partial(s_{2})=\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}(\partial s_{2})-\partial\nabla_{(\sharp^{C}\circ\partial)s_{2}}s_{1}=\\
\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}(\partial s_{2})-\partial\left(\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}-[s_{1},s_{2}]\right)=\partial[s_{1},s_{2}]+\left(\nabla_{s_{1}}^{C}\partial s_{2}-\partial\nabla^{A}_{s_{1}}s_{2}\right)=\partial[s_{1},s_{2}],\end{split}

by the compatibility
\nabla^{C}
with
\nabla^{A}
, so (

Lie
∂

) is true. It remains to prove that
\Box
satisfies (

Lie
□

), see Remark
3.23
.

\displaystyle\Box_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v

\displaystyle=\nabla^{C}_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}[s_{1},s_{2}],

\displaystyle\Box_{s_{1}}\Box_{s_{2}}v

\displaystyle=\Box_{s_{1}}\left(\nabla^{C}_{s_{2}}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}\right)=\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}\left(\nabla^{C}_{s_{2}}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}\right)-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}\Box_{s_{2}}v}s_{1},

hence, using (

Lie
∂

), we get

\begin{split}-\mathrm{curv}_{\Box}(s_{2},s_{2};v):=\Box_{[s_{1},s_{2}]}v-\Box_{s_{1}}\Box_{s_{2}}v+\Box_{s_{2}}\Box_{s_{1}}v=-\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{C}}(s_{1},s_{2};v)\\
-\partial\circ\left(\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}[s_{1},s_{2}]-\nabla_{[\sharp s_{2},\sharp^{C}v]}s_{1}+\nabla_{[\sharp s_{1},\sharp^{C}v]}s_{2}\right){+}\nabla^{C}_{s_{1}}(\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{2}){-}\nabla^{C}_{s_{2}}(\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}v}s_{1}).\end{split}

We replace
-\mathrm{curv}_{\nabla^{C}}(s_{1},s_{2};v)
with
\partial\circ K(s_{1},s_{2};v)=\partial\circ R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}v)
, see (
iii
), and
\nabla^{C}\circ\partial
with
\partial\circ\nabla^{A}
, and find that
-\mathrm{curv}_{\Box}(s_{2},s_{2};v)=\partial\circ\triangle(s_{1},s_{2};\sharp^{C}v)
, where

\triangle(s_{1},s_{2};X)=R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2};X)-\left(\nabla_{X}[s_{1},s_{2}]-\nabla_{[\sharp s_{2},X]}s_{1}+\nabla_{[\sharp s_{1},X]}s_{2}\right)+\left(\nabla_{s_{1}}^{A}\nabla_{X}s_{2}-\nabla^{A}_{s_{2}}\nabla_{X}s_{1}\right).

We expand the last bracket using the formula (
3.51
) for
\nabla^{A}
and replace
R^{\mathrm{bas}}_{\nabla}(s_{1},s_{2})(X)
with (
3.53
), and after cancelling similar terms we get

\begin{split}\triangle(s_{1},s_{2})(X)=-\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}M}X}s_{1}+\nabla_{\nabla_{s_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}M}X}s_{2}+\left(\nabla_{[\sharp s_{2},X]}s_{1}-\nabla_{[\sharp s_{1},X]}s_{2}\right)+\\
\left(\nabla_{\sharp\nabla_{X}s_{2}}s_{1}-\nabla_{\sharp\nabla_{X}s_{1}}s_{2}\right)=0\end{split}

thanks to the formula for
\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}
given in (
3.52
). The proof of (

Lie
□

) is completed. We have obtained the following result.

Theorem 3.26
.

Let
(A\to M,[\cdot,\cdot],\sharp)
be a Lie algebroid and let us fix a vector bundle
C\to M
, a linear connection
\nabla
on
A
and a VB morphism
\partial:A\to C
over
\operatorname{id}_{M}
.

Assume, in addition, that we are given a representation u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
on the complex
\partial:A\rightarrow C
, and a morphism
\Phi=(\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1})
from this to the adjoint representation
(A,\operatorname{ad}_{\nabla})
such that
\Phi_{1}=0
and
\Phi_{0}|_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
. Here,
\Phi_{i}\in\Omega^{i}(A;\operatorname{End}^{i}(A_{[0]}\oplus C_{[1]}))
,
i=0,1
, are the components of
\Phi
.

Then, there exists a unique HA structure on the graded bundle
E^{2}
constructed in Lemma
3.6
,
such that the representation u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
, and the morphism
\Phi
, described in Lemma
3.25
, are the given ones. This
establishes a one-to-one correspondence between order-two Lie HA structures on the graded bundle
E^{2}

and morphisms
\Phi
of the above form.

3.2.7
HAs, VB-alegbroids and representations up to homotopy

A brief account of VB-algebroids is given in Preliminaries.

Recall that the constructions from Definition
3.24
and the adjoint representation depend on the choice of a linear connection on the vector bundle
A\to M
. However, there is a way to avoid this choice. The motivation comes from description of 2-term representations in a framework of VB-algebroids, as discovered in
[
GSM10
]
. In this framework, the adjoint representation of
A
is the VB-algebroid
(\mathrm{T}A;\mathrm{T}M,A;M)
– the tangent prolongation of the algebroid
A
.

Corollary 3.27
.

A Lie HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
can be described by means of the following data:

(i)

a VB-algebroid structure on a DVB
D
whose side bundles are
C
and
A
and the core is also
A
;

(ii)

a VB-algebroid morphism
\Psi
from
D
to
\mathrm{T}A
(the adjoint representation of
A
) such that
\Psi
is the identity on the side bundle
A
and also on the core bundle
A
:

\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

\scriptstyle{\Psi}

\textstyle{\mathrm{T}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{A}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{T}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

(3.57)

We proceed with the proof by recalling the correspondence between 2-term representations and VB-algebroids. Details are nicely presented in
[
GSJLMM18
]
.

Let
(D;\sigma_{E},\sigma_{A};M)
be a DVB with the core
C
, as in (
2.2
).
As shown in
[
GSM10
]
, a VB-algebroid structure on the DVB
(D\rightarrow E;A\rightarrow M)
, as in (
2.2
), together with a horizontal lift
\theta_{A}:\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)\rightarrow\operatorname{\Gamma}^{\ell}_{E}(D)
, i.e., a splitting of the short exact sequence (
2.3
), gives rise to a representation u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
. (Recall that such horizontal lifts are in bijective correspondence with decompositions
D\rightarrow E\times_{M}A\times_{M}C
of the DVB
D
, and with the inclusions
\sigma:E\times_{M}A\rightarrow D
.) We shall review this construction. First of all, it is a representation on the 2-term complex
\partial:C\to E
, where
\partial
is the core of the anchor map
\sharp^{D}:D\to\mathrm{T}E
. (Note that
\widehat{D}=C
and
\widehat{\mathrm{T}E}=E
.) The
A
-connections on
C
and
E
, denoted by
\nabla^{\mathrm{core}}
and
\nabla_{a}^{\mathrm{side}}
are the following (see
[
GSJLMM18
]
):

\left(\nabla^{\mathrm{core}}_{a}c\right)^{\dagger}=[\theta_{A}(a),c^{\dagger}]_{D},\hskip 9.24994pt\widetilde{\nabla_{a}^{\mathrm{side}}}=\sharp_{D}(\theta_{A}(a)),

(3.58)

where
a\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
,
c\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
and
\xi\mapsto\widetilde{\xi}
denotes 1-1 correspondence between derivative endomorphism of
\operatorname{\Gamma}(E)
and linear vector fields on
E
. The last component,
A
-form
K\in\Omega^{2}(A;\operatorname{Hom}(E,C))
is defined as

K(a_{1},a_{2})=\theta_{A}([a_{1},a_{2}]_{A})-[\theta_{A}(a_{1}),\theta_{A}(a_{2})]_{D}.

(3.59)

A DVB morphism
\Psi
between decomposed vector bundles,
E\times_{M}A\times_{M}C\to E^{\prime}\times_{M}A^{\prime}\times_{M}C^{\prime}
, covering
\operatorname{id}_{M}
, is uniquely defined by restrictions of
\Psi
to the side bundles
A,E
and the core
C
and a 1-form
\chi\in\Omega^{1}(A,\operatorname{Hom}(E,C^{\prime}))=\operatorname{\Gamma}(A^{\ast}\otimes E^{\ast}\otimes C^{\prime})
,

\Psi(e,a,c)=(\Psi|_{E}(e),\Psi|_{A}(a),\Psi|_{C}(c)+\chi(a,e)).

(3.60)

If
A=A^{\prime}
and
\Psi|_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
then
\Psi
defines a graded VB morphism
\Phi_{0}:C_{[0]}\oplus E_{[1]}\rightarrow C^{\prime}_{[0]}\oplus E^{\prime}_{[1]}
,
\Phi_{0}=\Psi|_{C}\oplus\Psi|_{E}
. Note that
\operatorname{Hom}(E,C^{\prime})=\operatorname{Hom}_{-1}(C_{[0]}\oplus E_{[1]},C^{\prime}_{[0]}\oplus E^{\prime}_{[1]})
.

Theorem 3.28
.

Let
A\to M
be a Lie algebroid.

(i)

[
GSM10
]
Let
D
be a DVB as in (
2.2
), and
\theta_{A}
be a horizontal lift. (It gives rise to a decomposition
D\simeq E\times_{M}A\times_{M}C
.)
Then the formulas (
3.58
) and (
3.59
) establish a one-to-one correspondence between algebroid structures on
D\to E
that provide a VB-algebroid structure on the DVB
D
and 2-term representations u.t.h. of the Lie algebroid
A
on the complex
\partial:C\to E
.

(ii)

[
DJLO15
]
Let the decomposed DVBs
D:A\times_{M}E\times_{C}
,
D^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\times_{M}E^{\prime}\times_{C}^{\prime}
carry VB-algebroid structures and assume that the Lie algebroids
A
,
A^{\prime}
are the same. Then a DVB morphism
\Psi:D\to D^{\prime}
such that
\Psi|_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
, is a VB-alegbroid morphism if and only if
\Phi=(\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1})
, where
\Phi_{0}=\operatorname{id}_{A}\oplus\Psi|_{C}
, and
\Psi_{1}=\chi
, is a morphism between the associated 2-term representations.

Proof of Corollary
3.27
.

Let us assume that we are given a VB-algebroid morphism
\Psi
, as above. Denote
\sharp^{C}:=\Psi|_{C}:C\to\mathrm{T}M
. Let
\nabla
be any linear connection on
A
. This corresponds to a decomposition
\sum^{\nabla}:A\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}A
of the DVB
\mathrm{T}A
. Thanks to presence and properties of
\Psi
, the DVB
D
has a decomposition, induced by
\nabla
, as well. Indeed,
\Psi
is an affine bundle morphism from
D
to
\mathrm{T}A
covering
\underline{\Psi}=\operatorname{id}_{A}\times\sharp^{C}:A\times_{M}C\to A\times_{M}\mathrm{T}M
. It is fiber-wise bijective since
\Psi
is the identity on the core bundle
A
. Hence, there exists a unique decomposition
\sum^{D}:A\times_{M}C\to D
such that
\Phi\circ\sum^{D}=\sum^{\nabla}\circ\underline{\Phi}
.

In our case, the VB-algebroid structure on the DVB
D
, given in (
3.57
), induces a representation of the Lie algebroid
A
on the complex
\partial:A\to C
.
Besides,
\Psi
as a morphism of VB-algebroids, induces a morphism
\Phi=(\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1})
of 2-term representations, as described in Theorem
3.28
. In our case,
\Phi_{1}\in\Omega^{1}(A;\operatorname{Hom}(C,A))
vanishes, since
\Psi
respects the decompositions of
D
and
\mathrm{T}A
. Therefore,
\Phi
is of the form described in Theorem
3.26
, i.e.,
\Phi=(\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1})
,
\Phi_{1}=0
,
\Phi_{0}|_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
.

We shall prove that the
A
-connection on
A
in the complex
A\to C
is the same as in the adjoint representation.
According to (
3.58
), these
A
-connections on the core bundles of
D
and
\mathrm{T}A
, denoted by
\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(D)}
and
\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(\mathrm{T}A)}
, respectively, are given by

\left(\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(D)}_{a_{1}}a_{2}\right)^{\dagger}=[\theta^{D}_{A}(a_{1}),a_{2}^{\dagger}]_{D},\hskip 9.24994pt\left(\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(\mathrm{T}A)}_{a_{1}}a_{2}\right)^{\dagger}=[\theta^{D}_{A}(a_{1}),a_{2}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{T}A}

where
a_{1},a_{2}\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
.
We have
\Psi([\theta_{A}^{D}(a_{1}),a_{2}^{\dagger}]_{D})=[\theta_{A}^{\mathrm{T}A}(a_{1}),a_{2}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{T}A}
since
\Psi:D\to\mathrm{T}A
is a Lie algebroid morphism (covering the projection
E\to M
), the corresponding decompositions of DVBs
D
and
\mathrm{T}A
are
\Psi
-related (
\Psi\circ\theta^{D}_{A}=\theta_{A}^{\mathrm{T}A}
) and
\Psi
induces the identity on the core bundles. It follows that
\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(D)}=\nabla^{\mathrm{core}(\mathrm{T}A)}
.

Hence, due to Theorem
3.26
, we get a HA structure on a certain canonically constructed graded bundle
E^{2}
, defined in Lemma
3.6
.
We shall prove that the obtained HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
does not depend on the choice of the linear connection
\nabla
on
A
.
It amounts to showing that the structure map

\Box_{s}v=\nabla^{C}_{s}v-\partial\circ\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(v)}s

does not depends on the choice of
\nabla
. (The
A
-connection
\nabla^{C}
on
C\to M
associated with the VB-algebroid
(D;C,A;M)
in (
3.57
) is the
A
-connection denoted by
\Delta^{\mathrm{side}}
in (
3.58
).)
Let
\widetilde{\nabla}
be another linear connection on
A
, so
\widetilde{\nabla}-\nabla=:\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}(A\otimes\mathrm{T}M,A)
. From
[
GSJLMM18
, Remark 2.12]
, we find that

\widetilde{\nabla}^{C}_{s}v-\nabla^{C}_{s}v=\partial\circ\phi(s,v)\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(C),

where
\partial:A\to C
is as above and
\phi\in\operatorname{Hom}(A\otimes C,A)
is the difference of the decompositions of the DVB
D
induced by the linear connections
\widetilde{\nabla}
and
\nabla
. We have
\Phi(\sum^{D}(s,v))=\sum^{\mathrm{T}A}(s,\sharp^{C}(v))
, hence
\phi(s,v)=\varphi(s,\sharp^{C}(v))
. Moreover,

\partial\circ\left(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\sharp^{C}(v)}s-\nabla_{\sharp^{C}(v)}s\right)=\partial\circ\varphi(s,\sharp^{C}(v)),

what finishes the proof of our claim,
\widetilde{\Box}_{s}v=\Box_{s}v
, as
\widetilde{\Box}_{s}v-\Box_{s}v=\widetilde{\nabla}^{C}_{s}v-\nabla^{C}_{s}v
, see Definition
3.24
.
∎

Example 3.29
.

We shall describe the representation u.t.h. of
A
associated with the HA
(A^{[2]},\kappa^{[2]})
– the
2^{\mathrm{nd}}
prolongation of a Lie algebroid
(A\rightarrow M,[\cdot,\cdot],\sharp)
. From Example
3.8
, it follows from that this is a representation on the complex
\partial=\operatorname{id}_{A}:A\rightarrow{\widehat{A^{[2]}}}\simeq A
, and the
A
-connections defined in Definition
3.24
, denoted by
\nabla^{A}
and
\nabla^{C}
, coincide. Moreover, the 2-form
K
given in Definition
3.24
is the curvature of the
A
-connection
\nabla^{A}
. Indeed, we know from
[
AC12
]
that
\mathrm{curv}(\nabla^{A})={-}R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}\circ\sharp
while
K=R_{\nabla}^{\mathrm{bas}}\circ\sharp^{C}
, see (
3.54
), so
\mathrm{curv}(\nabla^{A})={-}K
as
\sharp=\sharp^{C}
in our case.

Now consider the linearisation
{\operatorname{lin}}(A^{[2]})
of the graded bundle
A^{[2]}
as a DVB, where we shall recognize a VB-algebroid structure and a morphism to the adjoint representation corresponding to the HA structure on
A^{[2]}
, as described in Corollary
3.27
.
It was shown in
[
BGG15b
, Theorem 2.3.8]
that
{\operatorname{lin}}(A^{[k]})\simeq A\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}A^{[k-1]}
and that it carries a natural weighted algebroid structure. In the special case
k=2
, we find that

{\operatorname{lin}}(A^{[2]})\simeq A\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}A=\{(a,X)\in A\times\mathrm{T}A:\sharp a=(\mathrm{T}\sigma)X\},

and the DVB
{\operatorname{lin}}(A^{[2]})
carries a canonical structure of a VB-algebroid. Note that the side bundles and the core of
{\operatorname{lin}}(A^{[2]})
are naturally identified with the VB
A\rightarrow M
.
Moreover, the Lie algebroid structure on the vector bundle
\operatorname{pr}_{1}:A\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}A\rightarrow A
, where
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
is the projection onto the first factor, is a special case of the construction called
the prolongation of a Lie algebroid
, see
[
Mar01
]
and
[
BGG16
]
.
The morphism
\Psi
of VB-algebroids has a straightforward form,
\Psi:A\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}A\rightarrow\mathrm{T}A
is induced by the projection onto the second factor:

\textstyle{A\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{A}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

\scriptstyle{\Psi}

\textstyle{\mathrm{T}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{A}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{T}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

We shall illustrate now the procedure of reconstructing an HA from a given representation a Lie algebroid and a morphism to the adjoint representation.

Example 3.30
.

We shall reconstruct a HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
out of the adjoint representation of a Lie algebroid
(\sigma:A\to M,[\cdot,\cdot],\sharp)
and the morphism
\Phi
being the identity on
A_{[0]}\oplus(\mathrm{T}M)_{[1]}
. According to Lemma
3.6
,
E^{2}
is the quotient
E^{2}={\raisebox{1.84995pt}{$A^{[2]}\times_{M}(\mathrm{T}M)_{[2]}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.84995pt}{$\sim$}\right.}
where the relation
\sim
is induced by the graph of
-\sharp:\widehat{A^{[2]}}\simeq A\rightarrow\mathrm{T}M=\widehat{T^{2}M}
. Note that order-one reduction of
E^{2}
is
A
, and its core is
\mathrm{T}M
. We can geometrically describe the graded bundle
E^{2}
as follows:

Take
(X,v)
and
(Y,w)
in
A^{[2]}\times_{M}(\mathrm{T}M)_{[2]}
. Then
(X,v)\sim(Y,w)
if and only if
\tau_{A}(X)=\tau_{A}(Y)
,
(\mathrm{T}\sigma)X=(\mathrm{T}\sigma)Y
, and
\sharp(Y-X)=v-w
, where
Y-X
is consider as an element of
A
via the isomorphism between
A
and
\mathbf{V}_{M}A\subset\mathrm{T}A
.
(Recall,
A^{[2]}
consists of
X\in\mathrm{T}A
such that
(\mathrm{T}\sigma)X=\sharp\tau_{A}(X)\in\mathrm{T}M
.)

The structure maps of the HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
are easy to describe:
\partial=\sharp
,
\sharp^{C}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{T}M}
and
\Box_{s}v=\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}_{s}v-\partial\nabla_{v}s=[\sharp s,v]
, according to the definition of
\nabla^{\mathrm{T}M}
, see (
3.52
). Since
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
is a Lie HA we have
\omega=0
, and
\beta(s_{1},s_{2})=\sharp[s_{1},s_{2}]
.

3.3
Final remarks and questions

The results presented in this paper (e.g. Theorems
3.26
,
3.15
) are the source of new examples of order-two graded bundles and HAs, eg. Example
3.30
, and raise questions about the classification of HAs under certain natural assumptions. This represents one potential direction for further development based on the findings of this paper.

Another avenue of research on HAs involves exploring how HAs of order

\geq 3
are related to representations u.t.h. of Lie algebroids.

In light of the paper
[
BO19
]
on the integration of 2-term representations of Lie algebroids, a natural question arises about the integration of HAs. What higher-order groupoids are and how they relate to HAs?

Recall that HAs were introduced as geometric-algebraic structures providing a proper language to formulate a geometric formalism of higher-order variational calculus (generalizing the first-order case). We hope this work will encourage further developments in the area of HAs and higher-order geometric mechanics.

4
Appendix

In what follows,
(x^{a},y^{i}_{w})
denotes graded coordinates on a graded bundle
E^{k}\rightarrow M
. In the case
k=2
,
we continue using the notation from Subsection
3.2
and Example
2.8
.
In particular,
A=E^{1}
,
C=\widehat{E^{2}}
, and
(e_{i})
,
(c_{\mu})
denote local frames of the VBs
A
and
C
, respectively;
(x^{a},y^{i},z^{\mu})
are graded coordinates on
E^{2}
compatible with the chosen frames
(e_{i})
,
(c_{\mu})
.

4.1
Vector fields of non-positive weight on graded bundles

In the following lemma, we study the structure of the space of vector fields of non-negative weight on a graded bundle
E^{k}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\sigma^{k}:E^{k}\rightarrow M
be a
graded bundle of order
k
and let
\mathfrak{X}_{\leq 0}(E^{k})=\bigoplus_{j=-k}^{0}\mathfrak{X}_{j}(E^{k})
denotes the Lie algebra of non-positively graded vector fields on
E^{k}
.

(i)

The Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E)
of
linear
vector fields on the total space
E
of a vector bundle
\sigma:E\rightarrow M
coincides with the Lie algebra of derivative endomorphisms of the dual bundle,
\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E)\simeq\mathfrak{D}({E}^{\ast})
.

(ii)

A vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{k})
of weight zero is projectable onto
E^{j}
for any
0\leq j\leq k
, in particular on
M=E^{0}
.

(iii)

\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{2})
is an abelian extension by
\operatorname{\Gamma}(\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Sym}^{2}E^{1},\widehat{E^{2}}))
of the Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{1})\oplus\mathfrak{X}_{0}(\widehat{E^{2}})
consisting of pairs
(X_{1},X_{2})
such that

X_{1}
and
X_{2}
project onto the same vector field on
M
:

0\to\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Sym}^{2}E^{1},\widehat{E^{2}})\to\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{2})\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{1})\times_{\mathfrak{X}(M)}\mathfrak{X}_{0}(\widehat{E^{2}})\to 0

(4.1)

where the projection
\pi
is given by
\pi(X)=((\mathrm{T}\sigma^{2}_{1})(X),X|_{\widehat{E^{2}}})
and the kernel of
\pi
can be canonically identified with the space of VB morphisms
\operatorname{Sym}^{2}E^{1}\to\widehat{E^{2}}
.

(iv)

There is a short exact sequence of graded Lie algebras

0\to\mathfrak{X}^{\mathbf{V}}_{<0}(E^{k})\to\mathfrak{X}_{<0}(E^{k})\to\mathfrak{X}_{<0}(E^{k-1})\to 0

(4.2)

where
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathbf{V}}_{<0}(E^{k})
denotes the subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{<0}(E^{k})
of those vector fields which are vertical with respect to the projection
\sigma^{k}_{k-1}:E^{k}\rightarrow E^{k-1}
.

(v)

In case
k=2
, the homogeneous part of weight
-1
of (
4.2
) reads as

0\to\mathfrak{X}^{\mathbf{V}}_{-1}(E^{2})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(E^{1},\widehat{E^{2}})\to\mathfrak{X}_{-1}(E^{2})\to\mathfrak{X}_{-1}(E^{1})\simeq\operatorname{\Gamma}(E^{1})\to 0

Proof.

Point (
i
) is well known, see e.g.
[
KSM02
]
or
[
EVT19
, Remark 2.1]
.
For the proof of (
ii
), write a vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{k})
in a general local form

X=f^{a}(x)\partial_{x^{a}}+f^{i}(x,y)\partial_{y^{i}_{w}},

where functions
f_{i}(x,y)
are homogenous of weight
w=\mathrm{w}(y^{i}_{w})
. It follows that the function
f_{i}(x,y)
does not depend on coordinates of weights greater than
\mathrm{w}(y^{i})
, so it is the pullback of a function on
E^{\mathrm{w}(i)}
.

Obviously,
\mathrm{T}\sigma^{k}
annihilates
\partial_{y^{i}_{w}}
and defines a projection of the vector field
X
onto
M
, which is
f_{a}(x)\partial_{x^{a}}
. A very similar proof works for the projections
\sigma^{k}_{j}:E^{k}\rightarrow E^{j}
where
j>0
.
For the proof of (
iii
) write
X\in\mathfrak{X}_{0}(E^{2})
in the form

X=f^{a}(x)\partial_{x^{a}}+f^{i}_{j}(x)y^{j}\partial_{y^{i}}+\left(f^{\mu}_{\nu}(x)z^{\nu}+f^{\mu}_{ij}(x)y^{i}y^{j}\right)\partial_{z_{\mu}}.

It follows that the vector field
X
restricted to the submanifold
\widehat{E^{2}}
is tangent to it and
X{|}_{\widehat{E^{2}}}=f^{a}(x)\partial_{x^{a}}+f^{\mu}_{\nu}(x)z^{\nu}\partial_{z_{\mu}}
. Besides,
(\mathrm{T}\sigma^{2}_{1})X=f^{a}(x)\partial_{x^{a}}+f^{i}_{j}(x)y^{j}\partial_{y^{i}}
, hence
X{|}_{\widehat{E^{2}}}
and
(\mathrm{T}\sigma^{2}_{1})X
project to the same vector field on
M
. Moreover, the kernel of the projection
\pi
consists of vector fields of the form
f^{\mu}_{ij}(x)y^{i}y^{j}\partial_{z_{\mu}}
which can be identified with a VB morphism from
\operatorname{Hom}(C^{\ast},\operatorname{Sym}^{2}(E^{1})^{\ast})=\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Sym}^{2}E^{1},C)
, where
C=\widehat{E^{2}}
.

For (
iv
) it is enough to notice that a vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}(E^{k})
of weight
\leq-1
has a well defined projection on
E^{k-1}
. Point (
v
) is a direct consequence of (iv).
∎

4.2
Leibniz-type identities of the structure maps of HAs

Proof of Lemma
3.12
.

Let
(e_{j})
and
(\underline{e}_{j})
be local frames of sections of the vector bundle
A\rightarrow M
, related by
e_{j}=T^{i}_{j}(x)\underline{e}_{i}
. Let
(c_{\mu})
be a frame of the vector bundle
C\rightarrow M
.
The graded bundle morphism
\Phi:A^{[2]}\to C_{[2]}
has the local form

\Phi(x^{a},y^{i},\dot{y}^{i})=(\Phi^{\mu}_{i}(x)\dot{y}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi^{\mu}_{ij}(x)y^{i}y^{j})c_{\mu},

where
\Phi^{\mu}_{ij}=\Phi^{\mu}_{ji}
.
On the other hand, a map
\Psi:\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)\times\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)\rightarrow\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
satisfying the Leibniz-type identity (
3.39
) is locally determined by the VB morphism
\rho
and local functions
\Psi^{\mu}_{ij}
, where
\Psi^{\mu}_{ij}=\Psi^{\mu}_{ji}
, as follows:

\Psi(e_{i},e_{j})=\Psi^{\mu}_{ij}(x)c_{\mu}.

In the given correspondence,
\rho
corresponds to the core VB morphism
\widehat{\Phi}:A\to C
, via the isomorphism
\widehat{A^{[2]}}\simeq A
. To complete the proof, we shall show that the change,
(e_{j})\mapsto(\underline{e}_{j})
, of local frames of
\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
results in the same transition functions for the local functions
(\Phi^{\mu}_{ij})
as for
(\Psi^{\mu}_{ij})
. By calculating the differential of
\underline{y}^{i}
, we find that the local coordinates
(x^{a},y^{i},\dot{y}^{i})
on
A^{[2]}
transform as
\underline{x}^{a}=x^{a}
,
\underline{y}^{i}=T^{i}_{j}y^{j}
,

\underline{\dot{y}}^{i}=T^{i}_{j}\dot{y}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{i}_{jk}y^{j}y^{k},\text{where }\alpha^{i}_{jk}=\frac{\partial T^{i}_{j}}{x^{a}}Q^{a}_{k}+\frac{\partial T^{i}_{k}}{x^{a}}Q^{a}_{j}.

It follows that if
\underline{\Phi}^{\mu}_{i}\underline{\dot{y}}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\Phi}^{\mu}_{ij}\underline{y}^{i}\underline{y}^{j}=\Phi^{\mu}_{j}\dot{y}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi^{\mu}_{jk}y^{j}y^{k}
then
\Phi^{\mu}_{j}=\underline{\Phi}^{\mu}_{i}T^{i}_{j}
, and

\Phi^{\mu}_{jk}=\underline{\Phi}^{\mu}_{i}\alpha^{i}_{jk}+\underline{\Phi}^{\mu}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}T^{j^{\prime}}_{j}T^{k^{\prime}}_{k}.

On the other hand,

\begin{split}\Psi(e_{j},e_{k})=\Psi(T^{j^{\prime}}_{j}\underline{e}_{j^{\prime}},T^{k^{\prime}}_{k}\underline{e}_{k^{\prime}})=T^{j^{\prime}}_{j}T^{k^{\prime}}_{k}\underline{\Psi}^{\mu}_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}c_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}(\sharp e_{j})(T^{k^{\prime}}_{k})\rho(\underline{e}_{k^{\prime}})+\frac{1}{2}(\sharp e_{k})(T^{j^{\prime}}_{j})\rho(\underline{e}_{j^{\prime}})=\\
c_{\mu}\left(T^{j^{\prime}}_{j}T^{k^{\prime}}_{k}\underline{\Psi}^{\mu}_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{jk}^{i}\underline{\rho}^{\mu}_{i}\right).\end{split}

Therefore, the transformations for
\Psi^{\mu}_{ij}
are the same as those for
\Phi^{\mu}_{ij}
, as we claimed. ∎

The following three lemmas concern the calculus with algebroid lifts introduced in (
2.29
).
The first one, Lemma
4.2
, is the most general – we do not assume any HA structure.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
M
be a smooth manifold.

(i)

If
X\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
,
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
then

{(\tau^{k}_{M})}^{*}X(f)=\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}(f^{(k)}),

(4.3)

where
{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}\in\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
is
(\mathrm{T}^{k}M,\kappa^{k}_{M})
-lift of
X
in weight
-k
.

(ii)

Let
\rho^{k}:E^{k}\to\mathrm{T}^{k}M
be any morphism of graded bundles covering
\operatorname{id}_{M}
. Let
v\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(\widehat{E^{k}})
,
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
. Then

({v^{\uparrow}})({(\rho^{k})}^{*}f^{(k)})=\widehat{\rho^{k}}(v)(f),

(4.4)

where
{v^{\uparrow}}
is the image of
v
in
\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(E^{k})
(see Lemma
2.6
) and
\widehat{\rho^{k}}(v)\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathrm{T}^{k}M})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(M)
is understood as a vector field on
M
thanks to the isomorphism
j^{k}_{M}:\mathrm{T}M\to\widehat{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}
given in (
2.7
).

Proof.

(
i
) Recall that the vector field
\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}
is constructed in two steps. First, we take the vertical lift
X^{(0)}\in\operatorname{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}(\mathrm{T}^{k}E)
of
X
where
E=\mathrm{T}M
, and then we compose it with
\kappa^{k}_{M}:\mathrm{T}^{k}\mathrm{T}M\rightarrow\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}^{k}M
, see (
2.29
). We shall describe
\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}
by means of its flow.

We take
E=\mathrm{T}M
and
\alpha=k
in (
2.25
) and read from (
2.26
) that the vertical lift
X^{(0)}
sends
[\gamma]_{k}\in\mathrm{T}^{k}M
to
[t\mapsto{\frac{1}{k!}}t^{k}X_{\gamma(t)}]_{k}\in\mathrm{T}^{k}_{\gamma(t)}\mathrm{T}M
. Hence, the vector field
\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
is given by

\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}_{[\gamma]_{k}}=[u\mapsto[t\mapsto\phi^{X}_{ut^{k}/k!}(\gamma(t))]_{k}]_{1}\in\mathrm{T}_{{[\gamma]_{k}}}\mathrm{T}^{k}M,

where
(t,x)\mapsto\phi^{X}_{t}(x)
,
x\in M
, is the flow of the vector field
X
. Hence,

\frac{1}{k!}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}(f^{(k)})=\left.{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}}\right|_{u=0}f^{(k)}([t\mapsto\frac{1}{k!}\phi^{X}_{ut^{k}/k!}(\gamma(t))]_{k})=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}\right|_{t=0}t^{k}/k!\,\left.{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}}\right|_{u=0}f(\phi^{X}_{u}(\gamma(t)))=\\
=\left.{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}}\right|_{u=0}f(\phi^{X}_{u}(\gamma(0)))=X(f)(\gamma(0))

as we claimed.

(
ii
):
First of all, note that the function
({v^{\uparrow}})({(\rho^{k})}^{*}f^{(k)})\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(E^{k})
has weight
-k+k=0
; hence, it is the pullback of a function on the base
M
, and it is enough to verify the equality (
4.4
) at a point
m\in M
.

The tangent vector
({v^{\uparrow}})_{m}\in\mathrm{T}_{m}E^{k}
is represented by the curve
t\mapsto t\cdot_{\widehat{E^{k}}}v_{m}
, which is equal to
h_{\sqrt[k]{t}}^{E^{k}}(v_{m})
if
t\geq 0
, where
h^{E^{k}}
is the homogeneity structure on
E^{k}
. Hence,

\text{LHS\ of \eqref{e:rhoVF}}=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right|_{t=0}f^{(k)}(\rho^{k}(t\cdot_{\widehat{E^{k}}}v_{m})).

Assume that the image of
\widehat{\rho^{k}}(v_{m})
in
\widehat{\mathrm{T}_{m}M}
is represented by a curve
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to M
,
\gamma(0)=m
, i.e.,

\text{RHS\ of \eqref{e:rhoVF}}=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\right|_{s=0}f(\gamma(s)).

Then
\rho^{k}(v_{m})=\widehat{\rho^{k}}(v_{m})=[s\mapsto\gamma(s^{k}/k!)]_{k}
as
k^{\mathrm{th}}
-velocity in
\widehat{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}\subset\mathrm{T}^{k}M
, hence

\rho^{k}(t\cdot_{\widehat{E^{k}}}v_{m})=t\cdot_{\widehat{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}}\rho^{k}(v_{m})=[s\mapsto\gamma(t\cdot s^{k}/k!)]_{k}.

Therefore,

\text{LHS\ of \eqref{e:rhoVF}}=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right|_{t=0}\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}s^{k}}\right|_{s=0}f(\gamma(ts^{k}/k!)).

Let
(x^{a})
be local coordinates on
M
around
m
such that
x^{a}(m)=0
. It is enough to prove (
4.4
) for
f=x^{a}
. If
\gamma^{a}(t):=x^{a}(\gamma(t))=c^{a}t+o(t)
then
\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}s^{k}}\right|_{s=0}\gamma^{a}(ts^{k}/k!)=c^{a}t+o(t)
. Hence, the left and right hand sides of (
4.4
) coincide with
c^{a}
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
(E^{k},\kappa^{k})
be an AL HA.

(i)

For
s\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E^{1})
and
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
, the following identities hold

\frac{1}{k!}{s}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}((\sharp^{k})^{\ast}f^{(k)})={(\sigma^{k})^{\ast}}\sharp^{1}(s)(f)=\frac{1}{{\alpha}!}{s}^{\langle{-{\alpha}}\rangle}((\sharp^{k})^{\ast}f^{({\alpha})})

for any
0\leq{\alpha}\leq k-1
.

(ii)

\sharp^{1}=\sharp^{\widehat{E^{k}}}\circ\partial^{k}
where
\sharp^{\widehat{E^{k}}}:\widehat{E^{k}}\rightarrow\mathrm{T}M
and
\partial^{k}:E^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{E^{k}}
are the VB morphisms given in Remark
3.9
.

Proof.

Proof of (
i
): Denote
X=\sharp^{1}s\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
for time being. We know from Theorem
2.11
that the vector fields
{s}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}
and
{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}
are
\sharp^{k}
-related, hence for any function
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
we have
{s}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}((\sharp^{k})^{\ast}\psi)=(\sharp^{k})^{\ast}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}(\psi)
. We take
\psi=f^{(k)}
, use (
4.3
) and get

\frac{1}{k!}{s}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}((\sharp^{k})^{\ast}f^{(k)})=\frac{1}{k!}(\sharp^{k})^{\ast}{X}^{\langle{-k}\rangle}(f^{(k)})=(\sharp^{k})^{\ast}(\tau^{k}_{M})^{\ast}X(f)=(\sigma^{k})^{\ast}X(f)

as
\sigma^{k}=\tau^{k}_{M}\circ\sharp^{k}
. This proves the first equality.

The second one follows from Lemma
2.14
. Indeed, consider the reduction of
(E^{k},\kappa^{k})
to weight
\alpha
. We find that the vector field
{s}^{\langle{-{\alpha}}\rangle}\in\mathfrak{X}_{-{\alpha}}(E^{k})
is projectable onto
E^{{\alpha}}
and its projection is

s^{\langle-{\alpha}\rangle_{\kappa^{{\alpha}}}}
hence the equality
(\sigma^{k})^{\ast}\sharp^{1}(s)(f)=\frac{1}{{\alpha}!}{s}^{\langle{-{\alpha}}\rangle}((\sharp^{k})^{\ast}f^{({\alpha})})
follows from the previous one by replacing
k
with
{\alpha}
.

Proof of (
ii
): The claim follows from the commutativity of the following diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{\Gamma}(E^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{E^{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\sharp}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(E^{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sharp^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\Gamma}(\widehat{E^{k}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\sharp^{k}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\Gamma}(\widehat{T^{k}M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}(M)}

where the arrow in the middle, labelled by
\sharp^{k}
, denotes a relation:
(X,Y)\in\sharp^{k}
if the vector fields
X\in\mathfrak{X}(E^{k})
and
Y\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
are
\sharp^{k}
-related. Actually, this relation restricted to the lowest degree
-k
becomes a mapping
\sharp^{k}:\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(E^{k})\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{-k}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
. Moreover,
\partial_{E^{k}}=\partial^{k}
and
{\partial_{\mathrm{T}^{k}M}}
are defined by means of algebroid lifts, as in Remark
3.9
. It follows from Lemma
2.16
that the composition of maps in the lower row is the identity on
\mathfrak{X}(M)
. All maps in the diagram are
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear, hence they give rise to VB morphisms. The square on the left is commutative due to Theorem
2.11
and the AL assumption. The square on the right is also commutative and it is a more general fact:
\sharp^{k}
can be replaced there with any graded bundle morphism
\rho^{k}:E^{k}\rightarrow F^{k}
, as stated in Lemma
2.6
.
∎

Recall, that for
k=2
, the formula (
2.31
) gives

\begin{split}{(fs)}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}&=f{s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}=2f\partial(s),\\
{(fs)}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}&=f{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}+({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}){s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle},\\
{(fs)}^{\langle{0}\rangle}&=f{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle}+({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}){s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}+\frac{1}{2}((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f}){s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}.\end{split}

(4.5)

We shall need the following lemma for proving tensor-like properties of some structure maps associated with a skew HA
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
be a skew HA. Let
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
,
s,s_{1},s_{2}\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(A)
and
v\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)\simeq\mathfrak{X}_{-2}(E^{2})
. Then

(i)

(\sharp^{C}v)(f)={v^{\uparrow}}((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f})
, where
{v^{\uparrow}}
is given in Lemma
2.6
,

(ii)

\frac{1}{2}{s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f})=(\sharp^{C}\circ\partial)(s)(f)
,

(iii)

(\sharp s)(f)={s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})={s}^{\langle{0}\rangle}(f)
,

(iv)

{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=\left(\sharp[s_{1},s_{2}]+\sharp s_{2}\circ\sharp s_{1}\right)(f)
.

Remark 4.5
.

We consider
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
and
{\sharp}^{*}(\dot{f})
as functions on
E^{2}
using the pullbacks of
f
by
\sigma^{2}:E^{2}\rightarrow M
and
\sigma^{2}_{1}:E^{2}\rightarrow E^{1}
, respectively. Note that if
(E^{2},\kappa^{2})
is AL, then
\sharp^{C}\circ\partial=\sharp
, hence (
ii
) coincides in this case with Lemma
4.3
(
i
) with
k=2
. It is tempting to add in (
iii
) the equality
(\sharp s)(f)=\frac{1}{2}{s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}(\left(\sharp^{2}\right)^{\ast}\ddot{f})
, but this requires the assumption (

AL
∂

), see Lemma
4.3
. In the AL case, point (
iv
) simplifies to
{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=\sharp s_{1}(\sharp s_{2}(f))
.

Proof.

In general, the
(\alpha)
-lift
f^{(\alpha)}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathrm{T}^{k}M)
has weight
0\leq\alpha\leq k
, the anchor map
\sharp^{k}:E^{k}\rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{k}M
preserves the gradings on
E^{k}
and
\mathrm{T}^{k}M
, while the vector field
{s}^{\langle{\beta}\rangle}\in\mathfrak{X}(E^{k})
, where
s\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E^{1})
, has weight
-k\leq\beta\leq 0
. Hence
{s}^{\langle{\beta}\rangle}(f^{(\alpha)})=0
whenever
\alpha+\beta<0
.

Clearly, the equation (
i
) is
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear in
v
, so it is enough to show (
i
) for
v
from a frame
(c_{\mu})
of local sections of
C\rightarrow M
. We have
\ddot{f}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}\ddot{x}^{a}+\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}\dot{x}^{a}\dot{x}^{b}
, so

(\sharp^{2})^{\ast}(\ddot{f})=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}\left(Q^{a}_{\mu}z^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}Q^{a}_{ij}y^{i}y^{j}\right)+{\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}Q^{a}_{i}Q^{b}_{j}y^{i}y^{j},}

(4.6)

hence
{c_{\mu}^{\uparrow}}((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}(\ddot{f}))=Q^{a}_{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}=\sharp^{C}(c_{\mu})(f)
, see (
3.14
), and (
i
) follows immediately.

Similarly, using (
4.5
), we find that (
ii
) and (
iii
) are
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear in
s
.
Thus it is enough to verify these equalities for
s=e_{k}
. This is straightforward: we use the formulas
(
3.18
)
for
{e_{k}}^{\langle{\alpha}\rangle}
and find that
\frac{1}{2}{e_{k}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f})=Q^{\mu}_{k}Q^{a}_{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}
, which coincides with
(\sharp^{C}\circ\partial)(e_{k})(f)=Q^{\mu}_{k}\sharp^{C}(c^{\mu})(f)
due to (
3.19
), thereby proving (
ii
). Next,
{\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}=\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}Q^{a}_{i}y^{i}
, and all three expressions in (
iii
) are equal to
Q^{a}_{k}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}
.

It remains to prove (
iv
). We claim that the left and right hand sides of (
iv
) are
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear in
s_{1}
, and their difference is also
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
-linear in
s_{2}
. Indeed, consider (
iv
) with
s_{1}
replaced with
gs_{1}
and expand
{(gs_{1})}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}
as in (
4.5
). Note that
{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})
has weight 1 and so it is killed by
{s_{1}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}
, hence
{(g\,s_{1})}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=g\,{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})
while

\sharp[gs_{1},s_{2}]+\sharp s_{2}\circ\sharp(gs_{1})=\left(g\sharp[s_{1},s_{2}]-(\sharp s_{2})(g)\,\sharp s_{1}\right)+\sharp s_{2}(g)\,\sharp s_{1})+g\sharp s_{2}\circ\sharp s_{1}=g\,\cdot\mathrm{RHS}_{\eqref{e:sharp_lift2ddf}}.

Similarly, using point (
iii
) and a weight argument, we obtain

\displaystyle{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{(gs_{2})}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})

\displaystyle={s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}\left(g{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}+{\sharp}^{*}(\dot{g}){s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}+\frac{1}{2}(\sharp^{2})^{\ast}(\ddot{g}){s_{2}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}\right)({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=

\displaystyle(g{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}+{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{g})\,{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle})({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=g\cdot\mathrm{LHS}_{\eqref{e:sharp_lift2ddf}}+(\sharp s_{1})(g)\cdot(\sharp s_{2})(f),

and, in the same way,
\left(\sharp[s_{1},gs_{2}]+\sharp(gs_{2})\circ(\sharp s_{1})\right)(f)=g\cdot\mathrm{RHS}_{\eqref{e:sharp_lift2ddf}}+(\sharp s_{1})(g)\cdot(\sharp s_{2})(f)
. It proves our claim, and thus it is enough to check (
iv
) with
s_{1}=e_{k^{\prime}}
and
s_{2}=e_{k}
. We have

{e_{k}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})={e_{k}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}Q^{a}_{i}y^{i}\right)=Q^{b}_{k}y^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{b}}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}Q^{a}_{i})+Q^{i}_{jk}Q^{a}_{i}y^{j}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}},

hence
\partial_{z^{\mu}}
kills above expressions. By applying
{e_{k^{\prime}}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}
we get

{e_{k^{\prime}}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{e_{k}}^{\langle{0}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f})=Q^{b}_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{b}}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}Q^{a}_{k^{\prime}})+Q^{i}_{{k^{\prime}}k}Q^{a}_{i}\partial_{x^{a}}=\sharp e_{k}(\sharp e_{k^{\prime}}(f))+(\sharp[e_{k^{\prime}},e_{k}])(f),

(4.7)

and we are done.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.11
and Theorem
3.13
part (a).

The formulas
\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{1}(s_{1},s_{2})=\beta(s_{1},s_{2})-\partial([s_{1},s_{2}])
and
{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})=\Box_{s_{1}}(\partial s_{2})-\partial([s_{1},s_{2}])
come from the definitions of the corresponding maps, compare (
3.35
) with (
3.16
), (
3.15
) and (
3.17
). The other properties of the maps
\varepsilon_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{1}
given in Lemma
3.11
follow immediately from the properties of the maps
\beta
and
\Box
given in Theorem
3.13
, which we are going to prove first.

•

Proof of (
•
‣
3.13
): As the Lie bracket of vector fields is skew symmetric, so
\beta(s_{1},s_{2})=-\beta(s_{2},s_{1})
. There are no vector fields on
E^{2}
of weight less than
-2
. Hence,
using (
4.5
), we get

{\beta}(s_{1},fs_{2})={\frac{1}{2}}[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},f{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]+{\frac{1}{2}}[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}){s_{2}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}]=\\
{\frac{1}{2}}f[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]+{\frac{1}{2}}{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}){s_{2}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}=f{\beta}(s_{1},s_{2})+(\sharp s_{1})(f)\partial(s_{2}),

(4.8)

by a weight argument and Lemma
4.4
(
iii
).

•

Proof of (
•
‣
3.13
) and (
•
‣
3.13
): We expand
{(fs)}^{\langle{0}\rangle}
as in (
4.5
)
and find that for
v\in\mathfrak{X}_{-2}(E^{2})\simeq\operatorname{\Gamma}(C)
and
s\in\operatorname{\Gamma}(E)
we have

\displaystyle\Box_{fs}v

\displaystyle=[f{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle},v]+{[({\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}){s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},v]}+[{\frac{1}{2}}(\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f}{s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle},v]=

\displaystyle=f[{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle},v]{-}v({\sharp^{2}}^{*}\ddot{f}){s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma \eqref{l:alifts_skew}}}}{{=}}f\cdot(\Box_{s}v)-(\sharp^{C}v)(f)\,\partial(s),

as
v(f)
,
v({\sharp}^{*}(\dot{f}))
,
[{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},v]
,
[{s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle},v]
vanish by inspecting weights.
Similarly for (
•
‣
3.13
):

\Box_{s}fv=[{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle},fv]=f[{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle},v]{+}{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle}(f)v=f(\Box_{s}v){+}(\sharp s)(f)v

by Lemma
4.4
(
iii
).

•

Proof of the properties of the map
\psi
given in Lemma
3.11
:

Let
h:=\frac{1}{2}(\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f}
for time being, so
h
is a function on
E^{2}
of weight two.
From (
4.5
) and the definition (
3.33
) of
\psi
we get

\left(\psi(gs_{1},s_{2})-g\psi(s_{1},s_{2})\right)(f)={\frac{1}{2}}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{g})\,{s_{1}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}(h)=0

by a weight argument, hence
\psi
is tensorial in its first argument. Also

\left(\psi(s_{1},gs_{2})-g\psi(s_{1},s_{2})\right)(f)={\frac{1}{2}}{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}\left(g\,{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}(h)+{\sharp}^{*}\dot{g}\,{s_{2}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}(h)\right)-{\frac{1}{2}}g{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}(h)\\
-(\sharp s_{1}\,g)\,(\sharp s_{2})(f)={\frac{1}{2}}{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}({\sharp}^{*}\dot{g})\cdot{s_{2}}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}(h)-(\sharp s_{1})(g)\,(\sharp s_{2})(f)=(\sharp s_{1})(g)\left(\sharp^{C}\circ\partial-\sharp\right){(s_{2})}(f),

by Lemma
4.4
(
ii
) and (
iii
), hence we get (
iii
).
Set
A(f)={s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}(h)
,
B(f)=(\sharp s_{1})\circ(\sharp s_{2})(f)
, so
\psi(s_{1},s_{2})=A-B
. By inspecting weights and using
(fg)^{(2)}=f\ddot{g}+\ddot{f}g+2\dot{f}\dot{g}
and Lemma
4.4
(
iii
) we find that

A(fg)=A(f)g+fA(g)+(\sharp s_{1})(f)(\sharp s_{2})(g)+(\sharp s_{1})(g)(\sharp s_{2})(f),

while

B(fg)=fB(g)+B(f)g+(\sharp s_{1})(f)(\sharp s_{2})(g)+(\sharp s_{1})(g)(\sharp s_{2})(f)

hence
\psi(s_{1},s_{2})
is a derivation. The coordinate formula (
3.36
) for
\psi(e_{k^{\prime}},e_{k})
follows directly from (
3.18
) and (
4.6
):

\displaystyle{e_{k^{\prime}}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{e_{k}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}(h)

\displaystyle={e_{k^{\prime}}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}\left(\frac{1}{2}Q^{\mu}_{ik}Q^{a}_{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}y^{i}+\frac{1}{2}Q^{a}_{ik}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}y^{i}+\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}Q^{a}_{i}Q^{b}_{k}y^{i}\right)=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(Q^{\mu}_{k^{\prime}k}Q^{a}_{\mu}+Q^{a}_{k^{\prime}k}\right)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}+\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}Q^{a}_{k^{\prime}}Q^{b}_{k},

\displaystyle(\sharp e_{k^{\prime}})(\sharp e_{k})(f)

\displaystyle=Q^{b}_{k^{\prime}}Q^{a}_{k}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}+Q^{b}_{k^{\prime}}\frac{\partial Q^{a}_{k}}{\partial x^{b}}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{a}}.

The formula (
3.38
) for
{\psi}^{\mathrm{sym}}
follows immediately from (
3.36
).
The skew-symmetric part of
\psi
is derived from (
3.33
):

{\psi}^{\mathrm{alt}}(s_{1},s_{2})(f)={\frac{1}{4}}[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]((\sharp^{2})^{\ast}\ddot{f})-{\frac{1}{2}}[\sharp s_{1},\sharp s_{2}](f)

and this coincides with the formula (
3.37
) due to the definition of
\beta
and Lemma
4.4
(
i
). The direct computation of
\sharp^{2}\circ\mathrm{\Theta}^{2}
using (
3.3
)
gives

\sharp^{2}\circ\mathrm{\Theta}^{2}(x^{a},y^{i},\dot{y}^{i})=\left(x^{a},\dot{x}^{a}=Q^{a}_{i}y^{i},\ddot{x}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}Q^{a}_{ij}y^{i}y^{j}+Q^{a}_{\mu}\left(Q^{\mu}_{i}\dot{y}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}Q^{\mu}_{(ij)}y^{i}y^{j}\right)\right)

and comparing it with

\sharp^{[2]}(x^{a},y^{i},\dot{y}^{i})=(x^{a},Q^{a}_{i}y^{i},\frac{1}{2}\widehat{Q}^{a}_{ij}\,y^{i}y^{j}+Q^{a}_{i}\,\dot{y}^{i})

as read from Example
3.8
, gives the desired equivalence:
\sharp^{2}\circ\mathrm{\Theta}^{2}=\sharp^{[2]}
if and only if
{\psi}^{\mathrm{sym}}=0
and
\sharp=\sharp^{C}\circ\partial
, see (
3.7
) and (
3.38
).

‣
3.13
):
The map
\delta
defined in (

) satisfies

\delta_{s}(fs_{1},s_{2})=f\delta_{s}(s_{1},s_{2})+(\sharp[s,s_{2}])(f)\,\partial(s_{1}),

(

)

\delta_{s}(s_{1},fs_{2})=f\delta_{s}(s_{1},s_{2})-(\sharp s)(f)\beta(s_{1},s_{2}){-(\sharp s_{1})(f)\,\Box_{s}\partial(s_{2})-(\sharp[s_{1},s]+\sharp s\circ\sharp s_{1})(f)\,\partial(s_{2})},

(

)

\delta_{fs}(s_{1},s_{2})=f\delta_{s}(s_{1},s_{2})+(\sharp s_{1})(f)\beta(s_{2},s)+(\sharp s_{2})(f)\beta(s_{1},s)+\left((\sharp s_{1})\circ(\sharp s_{2})+\psi(s_{1},s_{2})\right)(f)\,\partial(s).

(

)

where
\delta_{s}(s_{1},s_{2})=\delta(s_{1},s_{2},s)
.
We expand
{(f\,s)}^{\langle{0}\rangle}
as in (

we get

\displaystyle[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},[{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},f{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle}]]=f[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},[{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},{s}^{\langle{0}\rangle}]],

\displaystyle[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},[{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},{\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]]=[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},2{\sharp}^{*}\dot{f}\beta(s_{2},s)+\sharp s_{2}(f)\,{s}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}]=2\sharp s_{1}(f)\beta(s_{2},s)+2\sharp s_{2}(f)\beta(s_{1},s),

\displaystyle[{s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},[{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle},(\frac{1}{2}{(\sharp^{2})}^{*}(\ddot{f})){s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}]]={s_{1}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}{s_{2}}^{\langle{-1}\rangle}\frac{1}{2}\sharp_{2}^{\ast}(\ddot{f}){s}^{\langle{-2}\rangle}=\left(\psi(s_{1},s_{2})(f)+(\sharp s_{1})(\sharp s_{2})(f)\right)\,{}2\partial(s),

where
\psi
is defined in (
3.33
). Summing up these three equalities, we get (

3

δ

).
The equalities (

\displaystyle\omega_{s}(fs_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\sharp s_{1})(f)\varepsilon_{1}(s_{2},s)+\psi(s_{1},s_{2})(f)\,\partial(s),

and

,

,

and

are as in Definition

. From this, the equations (
